# Replicated Mode Production Readiness

Checked on June 10, 2026.

## Decision

Production replicated mode is a no-go for a user-facing release today.

The go decision is limited to continued development and internal qualification of the Ratis-backed replicated design behind the shaded or classpath/process adapter boundary.

## What Is Ready

- Apache Ratis remains the selected consensus substrate behind the adapter boundary.
- Java 25 JVM development and test execution are viable.
- Deterministic command encoding, version compatibility, state-machine replay, dedupe, crash recovery, backup/restore planning, CDC token handling, membership planning, and deterministic performance qualification have local test coverage.
- Unsupported replicated-mode APIs fail explicitly before entering the replicated log boundary.
- Baseline visible Netty native transport mediation is explicitly pinned to the project's Netty `4.2.12.Final` family.
- Packaging-boundary regression coverage now prevents accidental direct Ratis module-path dependencies in the production module descriptors, keeps Apache Ratis Maven artifacts out of the root project dependency graph, confines raw Ratis imports to adapter sources, locks the opt-in `ratis-spike` copy list and opt-in `ratis-shaded-adapter` relocation/resource/notice rules, requires central version pins, verifies generated notice metadata presence when adapter distributions exist, and blocks reintroduction of retired custom Raft/MicroRaft source packages or public protobuf RPCs.
- The first concrete external-process adapter lifecycle is implemented and tested: it launches a classpath-only child JVM command from a validated indexed or Maven-generated distribution manifest, rejects line-broken implementation names, unknown manifest keys, non-contiguous or non-canonical indexed classpath/JVM-argument entries, blank path-separated generated classpath entries, per-entry classpath separator injection, non-Java main-class names, mixed classpath formats, non-portable environment keys, module-path/classpath override arguments, Java launcher argument files, Java/native loader environment overrides, line-unsafe launch paths, NUL-bearing JVM or program arguments, and classpath entries outside the adapter home, reports the `EXTERNAL_PROCESS` boundary, verifies the bounded socket health probe request/response contract, requires a versioned `READY` health-check response before exposing a started process as running, and owns graceful plus forceful shutdown.
- Adapter distribution manifest, generated adapter metadata, and classpath adapter config readers now share a bounded UTF-8 properties loader that admits only no-follow regular files, rejects oversized file or byte payloads before `Properties.load`, and enforces the cap again while consuming file bytes, preventing symlink redirection or growth after the initial size check from bypassing startup validation; metadata re-encoding uses a bounded byte-array stream.
- A shared server-side `RatisServerLifecycle` wrapper now allows one adapter process lifecycle to be attached to multiple public endpoints without duplicate process starts, keeps it running if a later endpoint fails to start, and closes it after the last endpoint stops.
- The inactive `ratis-spike` compile now writes `target/ratis-adapter-distribution/` with a classpath adapter jar, copied runtime dependencies, a generated classpath file, and a generated `ratis-adapter.properties` manifest for the external-process command model.
- The selected `ratis-shaded-adapter` profile now starts a dedicated shaded adapter artifact path under `target/ratis-shaded-adapter-distribution/`: a single adapter jar with no external manifest `Class-Path`, a generated single-entry classpath file, generated adapter manifest, relocated Ratis packages, service/resource merge rules, module-descriptor/signature exclusions, copied Ratis/RainbowGum notice metadata for review, and an explicit shaded external-process rehearsal target.
- The first versioned process RPC codec for the adapter partition-operation boundary is implemented and tested for propose, wait-for-commit, linearizable query, stale query, local query, payload responses, commit acknowledgements, and adapter errors.
- The process RPC boundary now also carries an adapter-level `RELOAD_METADATA` command, with gateway-side client support, adapter-side dispatch without partition resolution, and a typed unsupported response when no metadata reloader has been wired.
- The process RPC bridge now includes a gateway-side external-process `RatisAdapterFactory`, socket transport/server, adapter-side dispatcher to `RatisPartitionClient`, request ID validation, malformed request handling, and adapter error mapping while preserving the Ratis classpath/process boundary.
- The inactive classpath adapter now includes Apache Ratis-backed partition client/server wrappers, an adapter runtime owner, and a metadata-driven Rockserver/RocksDB partition state-machine factory; the forked Ratis probe verifies Rockserver single-partition put/get/delete through one adapter runtime per local peer against real Ratis servers.
- The inactive adapter process entrypoint can now load strict indexed partition properties plus the generated metadata file, start adapter runtimes with real Rockserver/RocksDB state machines when metadata is provided, register the resulting Ratis-backed partition clients behind the socket RPC handler, and expose an optional adapter RPC listener alongside the existing health listener.
- The production-side process adapter config materializer can derive the classpath adapter's indexed partition properties from `ClusterMetadata` for the local voting partitions, using node Ratis endpoints/failure domains and rejecting local learner partitions until non-voting Ratis catch-up is proven.
- The production-side process adapter metadata codec can write/read the cluster metadata subset needed by adapter-side partition state-machine construction, including nodes, columns, partition descriptors, peers, placement constraints, key schema, and optional merge metadata, while rejecting malformed generated endpoint URIs.
- The production-side process adapter launch plan can publish the generated config and metadata files through temp-file writes and atomic replace when supported, build the classpath-isolated adapter command with fixed config/metadata/health/RPC arguments and reserved environment values, reject user or distribution attempts to override those reserved environment keys, reject non-portable, line-unsafe, NUL-bearing, Java-launcher-overriding, or native-loader-overriding environment entries before command/evidence materialization, reject line-unsafe JVM/program arguments and launch paths before launch, scrub inherited Java launcher and native loader environment overrides before process start, expose bounded preflight/evidence artifact publication for supervisor archival, expose a socket-health-checked lifecycle that requires an accepted deployment preflight before writing those files or starting the child process, and expose an RPC-backed `RatisAdapterFactory` without importing Apache Ratis into the production module.
- Replicated-mode config now exposes the adapter process launch settings, and `RatisProcessAdapterServerLifecycleFactory` can read the configured distribution manifest, derive generated config/metadata paths, produce a shared external-process lifecycle, and expose the matching RPC-backed adapter factory from `ReplicatedModeConfiguration` plus `ClusterMetadata`.
- `BootstrapPlan` can now be projected into initial `ClusterMetadata`, letting first-start adapter launch use bootstrap peer metadata before data partitions exist.
- `BootstrapManifestStore` provides byte-bounded, durably and strictly atomically published bootstrap manifest file round-trip and validation: successful writes force temp-file contents and containing-directory metadata, fail closed if atomic replacement is unavailable, enforce the runtime byte cap while parsing, require canonical peer indexes and bootstrap epoch values, require bare public endpoint shape and bare `grpc` Ratis endpoint shape, and feed the replicated-mode config `bootstrap-manifest-path` used by the server adapter factory's configured-bootstrap path.
- `NodeIdentityStore` now caps persisted identity reads and writes at 64 KiB, rejects oversized files before parsing, enforces the cap again while consuming file bytes so a growing identity file cannot bypass startup bounds, rejects non-regular identity paths instead of following symlinks, serializes first-start publication across threads and server processes with a lock file, and publishes the winning create-once identity through strict durable atomic replacement.
- Bootstrap metadata-group IDs now reject line-broken values at manifest construction and manifest-file load before they can feed internal Ratis metadata-group descriptor generation.
- `Main` now attaches the configured shared bootstrap Ratis lifecycle to both public server builders when replicated mode is enabled, so the adapter process starts before the public endpoints and closes after them.
- The adapter-backed client resolver can expose one RPC-backed `RatisPartitionClient` map through the write, point-read, and range-read route submitter resolver interfaces.
- Replicated server startup now prefers a strict existing generated adapter metadata file over bootstrap metadata, so restart-time adapter launch plans, lifecycles, adapter resolvers, and public `ReplicatedConnection` instances can be instantiated from the latest materialized metadata rather than the first-start bootstrap manifest; only a true missing generated file permits bootstrap fallback, while symlink or other non-regular paths fail closed.
- The adapter-backed client resolver now supports explicit partition refresh for running public API metadata updates: unchanged partition clients are retained, added partitions open clients, removed or changed descriptors close stale clients, duplicate refresh inputs fail before mutation, a failed refresh leaves the old route cache usable, and newly opened client cleanup failures are attached as suppressed failures instead of hiding the primary refresh failure.
- The classpath adapter now wires `RELOAD_METADATA` to reread its generated config and metadata files after a fresh deployment preflight, validate the new snapshot, refresh unchanged Rockserver state machines in place, start added partitions before exposing their clients, close removed partitions, and reject descriptor changes for running partitions until partition membership workflows own those transitions.
- Generated adapter config-pair writes and complete committed-metadata refresh publication are now serialized within the server JVM, the indexed adapter config carries its matching metadata epoch, and the classpath runtime rejects a config/metadata epoch mismatch before startup or live reload can publish a mixed snapshot.
- Committed adapter metadata publication now rejects cluster switches, epoch regressions, and same-epoch changes to derived adapter partition config before generated-file replacement or reload while preserving idempotent retries and modeled same-epoch CDC token-translation publication.
- Classpath metadata-group snapshot publication now uses the shared durable atomic writer, replacing fixed temp files and weaker non-atomic move fallback with randomized temp files, file forcing, strict atomic replacement, durable parent preparation, and post-rename directory forcing before Ratis records the snapshot.
- The classpath Rockserver state-machine factory now rejects data-partition descriptors whose Ratis group ID or ordered voter topology differs from generated metadata before startup or reload, preventing direct config-file mutation from silently launching an inconsistent consensus topology.
- Classpath adapter config loads now require every partition descriptor local peer ID to match the launch plan's trusted `ROCKSERVER_NODE_ID` environment value before startup or reload, preventing direct config-file mutation from making a child JVM bind as another valid voter.
- Adapter launch plans now reserve trusted `ROCKSERVER_RATIS_DATA_ROOT`, deployment preflight checks that value, and classpath adapter config loads require each descriptor path to equal its canonical `metadata-group` or `partition-<id>` location under that root before startup or reload, preventing direct config-file mutation from redirecting Ratis or RocksDB storage.
- Adapter launch plans now reserve trusted `ROCKSERVER_RATIS_METADATA_GROUP_TOPOLOGY_SHA256`, deployment preflight checks that value, and classpath adapter config loads match the parent fingerprint of metadata-group absence or immutable group ID and ordered voters before startup or reload, preventing direct config-file mutation from silently replacing bootstrap consensus topology.
- Canonical adapter data-path construction is now shared by generation, deployment preflight, and classpath loads; partition IDs containing path separators are rejected before normalization, and preflight reserves the complete adapter data root against non-canonical descriptors or config, metadata, and working-directory overlap so mutable IDs cannot redirect storage outside the trusted root or occupy future partition namespace.
- Parent preflight and child classpath config loads now share symlink-aware existing-ancestor path resolution and require canonical partition directories to resolve inside the trusted adapter data root, rejecting escaped child symlinks while preserving explicitly configured symlinked roots whose children remain contained.
- Generated adapter config, metadata, preflight-report, and deployment-evidence writes now force completed temp-file contents and metadata before rename, then force the containing directory after rename, so successful atomic replacement is durable across a crash; focused tests preserve existing targets and clean temp files on bounded-write failure and replace target symlinks without mutating their destinations.
- Generated adapter file publication now requires atomic replacement and fails closed when the filesystem cannot provide it; it no longer falls back to a weaker non-atomic move that could expose partial config-pair state.
- Trusted adapter storage containment now resolves existing ancestors strictly for authoritative root and deployment-overlap checks: broken symlinks, inaccessible entries, and other resolution failures reject parent preflight or child config loads instead of falling back to lexical containment.
- Generated-file publication, deployment preflight, and direct adapter process startup now prepare directories durably: when nested parents are newly created, they force each new directory and the existing ancestor that received the top-level entry before continuing, so a successful generated-file rename does not rely on unflushed parent-directory ancestry.
- Generated adapter artifacts and bootstrap manifests now share one internal durable atomic file writer, keeping strict atomic replacement, temp-file forcing, post-rename containing-directory forcing, and durable directory-ancestry preparation aligned across both publication surfaces.
- Generated adapter config/metadata pair publication now stages metadata first and atomically replaces config last as the activation file. Config includes the canonical semantic metadata-properties SHA-256, and classpath adapter startup or reload requires both digest and metadata-epoch equality before constructing resources, so interrupted publication or tampering fails closed instead of loading a mixed pair.
- Generated adapter config-pair publication now combines a reference-counted, lock-path-scoped JVM monitor with a no-follow sidecar file lock, serializing metadata staging and config activation across parent threads or distinct parent JVMs that share generated paths without serializing unrelated generated config pairs during startup or reload activation. Lock-path collision with metadata is rejected before publication.
- Generated adapter config-pair publication now reads the existing sidecar once while holding that file lock and rejects cluster changes or metadata-epoch regression before staging. Factory-owned refresh and process startup run same-epoch adapter-partition admission in the same critical section, so stale plans cannot overwrite a newer pair after waiting behind another supervisor.
- Generated adapter config-pair locking now remains held through post-publication activation: process startup includes child launch and health confirmation, while live metadata refresh includes the child `RELOAD_METADATA` RPC and resolver partition refresh. A second supervisor cannot interleave a newer pair between activation and parent consumption.
- Configured restart now reads generated config and metadata together under the pair lock, falls back to bootstrap only when both artifacts are absent, and accepts generated metadata only when config's activated canonical semantic digest matches the sidecar. Metadata-only staging, config-only remnants, and digest-divergent sidecars fail closed instead of being promoted during restart.
- The forked external-process rehearsal tampers a live node's metadata sidecar with a valid same-epoch but digest-divergent snapshot, verifies the real `RELOAD_METADATA` RPC rejects it while existing reads remain available, restores the matching sidecar, and then verifies reload succeeds.
- Forked external-process rehearsal partition-client helpers now retain and close each temporary RPC adapter factory with its client, including rollback close if partition opening fails, so repeated proof phases do not leak factory-owned socket transports.
- Committed adapter metadata refresh remains replayable across both publication phases: a retry repairs metadata staged before failed config activation, and a published pair whose adapter reload failed can be replayed before resolver exposure.
- Bootstrap manifest and node-identity reads now admit only no-follow regular files and open them with `NOFOLLOW_LINKS`, so first-start cluster topology and persisted identity cannot be redirected through symlinks between admission and parsing.
- Deployment preflight config/metadata distinctness, generated-file containment, working/data directory separation, and pairwise partition-directory overlap now use strict existing-ancestor resolution and report filesystem resolution failures explicitly instead of accepting lexical comparisons.
- Deployment preflight now requires the trusted adapter distribution root to resolve before classpath admission; a missing or otherwise unresolvable `adapterHome` is an explicit startup error instead of skipping classpath containment checks.
- Deployment evidence launch-file hashing now resolves each executable or classpath target once, admits only a regular target up to 1 GiB, hashes exactly the admitted byte count, and verifies the resolved target metadata afterward, rejecting oversized files, detectable growth, truncation, or replacement instead of archiving an unstable fingerprint.
- Deployment evidence now reads the published generated config and metadata files under the launch-plan config lock, rejects missing or tampered generated files before archival, and records both planned and published config/metadata hashes. The configured evidence helper verifies preflight before publishing generated config, so rejected distributions do not leave generated config behind.
- The inactive classpath probe now starts three external adapter JVMs from the generated adapter distribution, runs Rockserver put/read traffic through process RPC, transfers leadership to a selected peer, restarts that peer from retained storage, writes and reads through another peer after the restart, and verifies the restarted peer catches up enough to serve a stale/local read.
- A deterministic metadata-command applier now covers the metadata fields modeled in `ClusterMetadata`, including partition split/merge changes to partition and route-group state, CDC token translation records, schema-change prepare/activate/fence state, and backup-manifest records, and the modeled metadata commands have a checksummed, size-bounded log payload codec. `ReplicatedConnection` can build public `createColumn`/`deleteColumn` metadata commands and submit planned split/merge topology plus CDC token translation commands when a metadata submitter is injected, and the reusable metadata-log submitter can propose encoded commands to a `ReplicatedLogClient` and read back the committed metadata snapshot. On committed metadata refresh, the process adapter path republishes generated config/metadata files, sends adapter-level `RELOAD_METADATA`, and refreshes resolver clients only after reload succeeds.
- Synchronous metadata-log submission and adapter metadata refresh preserve fatal collaborator severity: `Error` causes from asynchronous completion are rethrown directly rather than downgraded to `CompletionException`, runtime causes retain direct propagation, and checked causes remain wrapped.
- The partition write command codec and its nested replicated mutation codec now bound encoded command/mutation bodies and key/value byte fields, rejecting malformed inbound lengths before large allocation while preserving the checksummed write envelope and replay path.
- Replicated partition writes now seal each calculated storage key plus the admitted metadata epoch, partition ID, partition epoch, and calculated-key range before proposal; replay materializes RocksDB writes and CDC events from that immutable admission context instead of recalculating against newer metadata, rejects missing, mismatched, or out-of-range seals, and keeps legacy unsealed payloads replayable only while their original schema epoch remains active.
- Gateway write submission and per-partition admission now accept commands rather than caller-supplied command/payload pairs: the trusted boundary checks direct admission deadlines and metadata freshness before sealing, rejects future write route epochs before sealing or resolver dispatch, wraps rejected `NOT_LEADER` refreshed metadata as a typed write-route failure, seals routing context, derives checksummed payload bytes internally, reuses those stable bytes across retries, and rejects direct queue insertion of unsealed commands, preventing internal callers from routing or queueing one write while proposing unrelated bytes.
- Per-partition write admission now rechecks deadlines after route planning and before queue lookup, preventing failed deadline admissions from creating empty per-partition queues, while admitted records retain the caller deadline for downstream dispatch.
- Draining the final admitted write from a per-partition admission queue now prunes that queue from the controller registry, so workers and telemetry see only partitions with pending work instead of stale empty partition IDs after successful dispatch.
- Per-partition admission queues now prune expired queued writes before capacity checks, queue-depth accounting, partition-set reporting, and batch drains, so timed-out writes do not hold capacity, keep stale partitions visible, or dispatch after their caller budget has expired.
- Partition route-cache refresh now preserves cached route targets for same-epoch metadata updates whose shard routing data is unchanged, while still updating the metadata view for route-neutral records such as CDC token translations. Pre-commit metadata-refresh listener failures leave the previous metadata and cached routes active.
- Real classpath data-partition state machines now validate leader-side Ratis proposals before append: new proposals must carry a seal whose admitted routing context still matches the active partition, while the separate apply/replay path retains bounded legacy unsealed replay compatibility for historical log entries.
- Gateway point-read routing and real classpath data-partition state machines now share one core point-read validator for column schema epochs and calculated keys. Replica-local queries reject keys outside the local half-open partition range before RocksDB access, so opaque adapter requests cannot bypass gateway ownership routing.
- Partition point-read and range-read submitters now distinguish checked query failures from deadline or timeout exhaustion with `QUERY_FAILED`, while still preserving runtime and fatal collaborator severity directly. Unsupported stale/local point-read adapter modes also return typed `QUERY_FAILED` route failures with policy-specific diagnostics. Unsupported linearizable range-query adapter modes return typed `QUERY_FAILED` route failures for both synchronous and future-completed unsupported operations. Direct write admission and read submitters distinguish future expected metadata epochs from stale epochs before queueing, resolving clients, or dispatching adapter work.
- Point-read deadline failures now report the selected routing policy before adapter resolution, so expired linearizable, follower, bounded-staleness, and local reads do not collapse into a misleading linearizable diagnostic.
- Range-scan deadline failures now distinguish initial pre-routing validation from per-segment dispatch checks, so segmented scans and cursor pages do not report a stale "before routing" diagnostic after route planning has already succeeded.
- Linearizable read routing now requires the selected leader to be serving reads and caught up to any requested minimum applied index, while write and metadata routing still target the leader even when it is not serving reads.
- Partition read/write submitters now fail closed when adapter resolvers, returned futures, or successful write proposal commits violate the non-null contract, or when process adapter RPC calls complete with adapter error responses: point and range reads report typed `QUERY_FAILED` route failures that the public connection maps to `GET_1`, while writes report typed `RETRY_EXHAUSTED` route failures that the public connection maps to `COMMIT_FAILED_TRY_AGAIN`.
- Partition point/range read routing now reports stale or future route-cache epoch mismatches as typed `STALE_ROUTE` failures, wraps read-policy and partition-boundary routing failures such as missing caught-up replicas, non-serving leaders, out-of-range point keys, non-contiguous range metadata, or splitting scan partitions as typed `QUERY_FAILED` route errors, and read submitters map resolver runtime failures to typed `QUERY_FAILED` route errors instead of leaking raw collaborator exceptions. Malformed range bounds remain request validation failures.
- Partition write submission now handles synchronously thrown proposal failures inside the same retry boundary as future-completed failures: retryable `NOT_LEADER` failures refresh metadata and retry the stable encoded payload, while fatal proposal `Error`s keep their direct severity.
- Partition write routing now reports stale or future route-cache epoch mismatches as typed `STALE_ROUTE` failures, reports non-epoch write-routing failures such as missing leaders or out-of-range write keys as non-retryable typed `ROUTE_FAILED` failures, and write submission handles pre-proposal route/resolve failures inside the same retry boundary as proposal failures, preventing concurrent metadata refresh from leaking raw state exceptions.
- Replicated async range streams now execute blocking range iteration through the bounded replicated async executor, map executor saturation to a deterministic public async failure, cancel pending dispatch when subscribers dispose the stream, and reject subscriptions after close before adapter dispatch.
- Replicated async metadata-only reads now bypass executor admission: column ID lookup, column-definition snapshots, merge-operator metadata checks, and `RequestType.none()` point reads complete without consuming bounded worker capacity or dispatching adapter work.
- Replicated async range/reduce-range requests now reject negative timeout durations before executor admission, so malformed requests do not turn into saturation failures or consume adapter-routing capacity.
- Real classpath metadata-group state machines now reject malformed metadata proposals in `startTransaction(...)` and validate stateful transitions in Ratis' strictly serialized `preAppendTransaction(...)` hook against a leader-local projected metadata view. The projection resets from committed state across leadership changes, same-epoch concurrent conflicts fail before append, and metadata proposals are capped at 1 MiB below the explicitly configured 4 MiB Ratis append buffer.
- The obsolete main-source `ReplicatedWriteSyncAPI` facade has been removed: its raw-mutation proposal behavior now exists only as the test-only `LegacyReplicatedWriteSyncFixture`, preventing accidental production wiring around metadata routing, sealing, request-ID dedupe, and persisted response semantics while preserving classifier and transport-boundary fixture coverage.
- The persisted partition write response codec now bounds encoded response bodies, nested buffer fields, and caller-specific response counts, rejecting malformed inbound lengths or unexpected point-read response fanout before large allocation while preserving dedupe response storage and retry replay.
- Persistent partition duplicate-request replay now decodes stored dedupe responses with the current command's exact expected response count before advancing `lastApplied`, so a malformed or mismatched stored response payload cannot expand to the global codec maximum or publish duplicate progress before validation.
- Response-producing replicated public sync and async single-request writes now route through the production partition-write bridge with deterministic response modes for `put`, `delete`, `merge`, and their multi-key variants, decode the persisted committed response payload before returning, preserve null previous values in multi responses, and fail closed if a committed response payload is missing or malformed.
- Replicated public writes, point reads, and range reads now resolve unknown column IDs through a shared typed metadata lookup before partition routing, returning `COLUMN_NOT_FOUND` without dispatching to the adapter instead of leaking unchecked metadata validation exceptions.
- Replicated public column metadata commands now preserve public error types when the committed metadata log rejects a stale gateway view for duplicate create-column names or missing delete-column targets, returning `COLUMN_EXISTS` or `COLUMN_NOT_FOUND` instead of collapsing those deterministic conflicts into generic commit failures. Post-commit metadata refresh or listener failures remain generic commit retry failures instead of being misclassified as column conflicts.
- Replicated public metadata workflows now verify that the committed metadata snapshot contains each submitted command's expected effect before refreshing the route cache, so a faulty metadata submitter cannot report success with a valid but unchanged snapshot that omits node registration, partition creation, membership, topology, schema-change, backup-manifest, or CDC-translation effects.
- Replicated public split/merge topology workflows now treat the topology command and paired CDC token translation as one local route-refresh boundary: the gateway refreshes committed metadata only after the CDC translation commit is verified, so a failed translation cannot expose half-visible child or merged partition routes.
- The point-read and range-read query command codecs now bound encoded query bodies and key-part byte fields, rejecting malformed inbound lengths before large allocation while preserving the Ratis read-query routing path.
- The partition range-read response codec now bounds encoded result bodies, key parts, value fields, and caller-specific entry counts while preserving ordered `KV` results for fixed-key replicated `allInRange`, `entriesCount`, and `firstAndLast` reads. Classpath data-partition state machines answer range query envelopes by scanning only the local user-data column key range and fail closed on variable-tail columns or segment entry overflow; the replicated gateway independently rejects segment responses that exceed the requested entry limit.
- Replicated `checkMergeOperator` now uses committed metadata to recognize active merge-operator artifact hashes without dispatching to data partitions or uploading artifacts. It only returns a version when an activated or fenced merge-operator schema change still matches the current column schema, and `uploadMergeOperator` fails before metadata submission until replicated artifact distribution and activation preflight exist.
- Replicated public multi-key writes now cap each expanded command at `PartitionWriteCommand.MAX_MUTATIONS` (16,384) before proposal, and partition write decode applies the same limit to nested mutation batches before list preallocation. `putBatch` and `mergeBatch` accept only safe `WRITE_BATCH`/`MERGE_WRITE_BATCH` publishers, expand completed batches into deterministic ordered mutation lists, and leave no-WAL/SST ingestion modes rejected until replicated manifests exist.
- Cross-partition replicated multi-key writes now fail as an explicit first-cut unsupported admission error before partition resolver or proposal dispatch. The same typed admission failure is used by the submitter and queue admission paths, and the public replicated connection maps it to `NOT_IMPLEMENTED` rather than leaking an unchecked validation exception.
- The stateless range-scan cursor codec now bounds encoded cursor bodies and embedded range-command fields, rejecting malformed inbound lengths before large allocation while preserving cursor resume semantics without server-side iterator handles.
- The replicated CDC event codec now bounds encoded event bodies and key/value byte fields, rejecting malformed inbound lengths before large allocation while preserving committed CDC persistence, stream merge, and backup/restore paths.
- Authenticated metadata, mutation, write-command, write-response, CDC-event, read-command, and scan-cursor codecs now reject declared field lengths that exceed the bytes remaining in the decoded body before `readNBytes` allocation; collection-bearing codecs also reject counts that cannot fit the remaining body before preallocation, and persisted write responses cap nested delta depth.
- gRPC and Thrift client/server transport mappers now reject oversized key-part counts, batch/multi-entry counts, and unrecognized gRPC schema hash enum values before array/list allocation or unchecked runtime failures, while shared Java API default request handlers return typed `NOT_IMPLEMENTED` failures for unsupported sync, future, and Publisher requests instead of raw unsupported-operation exceptions.
- Public client and server builders now reject unsupported Thrift endpoint combinations with `IllegalArgumentException` and missing connection/listen types with `IllegalStateException`, so configuration admission fails before endpoint construction without raw unsupported-operation exceptions.
- Embedded column registration now maps duplicate internal name-admission failures to the public `COLUMN_EXISTS` error type, keeping idempotent and concurrent column creation aligned with public duplicate-column semantics instead of leaking raw unsupported-operation exceptions.
- `FIXEDINTEGER32` variable-tail hash admission now rejects non-four-byte inputs with public `UNSUPPORTED_HASH_SIZE` errors, preserving bucketed-key encoding behavior without leaking raw unsupported-operation exceptions.
- Replicated range-read streams preserve the standard exhausted `Stream` iterator contract for callers, while the owned range-read iterator's direct exhaustion path now carries an explicit diagnostic and the lazy multi-partition stream test covers post-exhaustion `next()` calls.
- The internal transactional database wrapper factory now rejects plain non-transactional RocksDB handles with `IllegalArgumentException`, preserving normal transaction behavior while avoiding raw unsupported-operation exceptions for factory misuse.
- Ratis feature-unavailable defaults now use a domain-specific `RatisFeatureUnavailableException`; point/range read submitters still map unavailable stale/local modes to typed route failures, and the process RPC handler still maps unavailable adapter features to `UNSUPPORTED` responses without depending on raw JDK unsupported-operation exceptions.
- Ratis adapter config and deployment-preflight validation no longer rely on bare production `Optional.orElseThrow()` calls after local-peer lookup, keeping missing-peer and local-learner diagnostics on explicit validation paths.
- gRPC streaming `putBatch` and `mergeBatch` endpoints now normalize unrecognized batch-mode enum values to typed `PUT_INVALID_REQUEST` Rockserver errors instead of leaking generic runtime failures for malformed protobuf requests.
- Cluster CDC page merging now rejects non-positive and oversized page requests before result-list allocation while preserving deterministic partition-local merge ordering.
- Partition backup artifact checksums now validate file-chunk CRC32C values against the chunk bytes and stream snapshot descriptor bytes directly into CRC32C instead of materializing an intermediate aggregate byte array, preserving the backup/restore checksum contract with lower memory pressure on large backups.
- Partition backup export now rejects manifests above a configured partition-count limit, keeps only the configured partition concurrency in flight, rejects oversized snapshot chunk lists before immutable copying, and computes bounded byte totals without cloning chunk payloads solely to measure them.
- Partition backup export now interrupts and bounded-waits for peer exporters before returning after success or failure, preserving the primary export failure while suppressing cleanup diagnostics if peer work cannot stop promptly.
- Partition backup export now keeps checked exporter failures tied to the affected partition, reports top-level export interruption explicitly, and restores exporter worker interrupt status without wrapping runtime or fatal exporter failures into lower-severity exceptions.
- Backup restore planning now validates target partition epoch entries before copying, caps manifest/artifact fan-out, snapshot chunk counts, and byte totals before checksum traversal or installer side effects, then recomputes aggregate snapshot CRC32C values from supplied chunks and rejects file-chunk byte/CRC mismatches before installer side effects.
- RocksDB checkpoint snapshot chunks, partition backup artifacts, backup restore plans, and Ratis partition snapshot manifests now reject out-of-range CRC32C values at construction, so impossible checksum metadata cannot enter snapshot installation or backup/restore validation paths.
- RocksDB checkpoint and outer Ratis snapshot manifests now cap chunk sizing at 64 MiB; checkpoint restore rejects negative commit indexes before checkpoint work, oversized or short intermediate chunks before acceptance, and paths that normalize to the install directory.
- RocksDB checkpoint snapshot generation and restore now apply configurable file-count, total-byte, and chunk-count limits with bounded defaults before restore grouping or writes and before oversized checkpoint publication; generation admits only no-follow regular checkpoint files, records their bounded sizes once, emits exactly those admitted byte counts, rejects truncation or growth, and rescans checkpoint metadata before success so filesystem drift cannot expand or silently reshape the snapshot after admission; restore also measures supplied chunk payloads with overflow-safe addition and rejects actual byte excess, manifest mismatch, invalid chunk indexes/counts/sizing, or checksum failures before creating its temporary install directory, then streams constructor-cloned immutable chunk payloads without a second per-chunk clone.
- Closed RocksDB snapshot installation now creates destination parents durably, rejects non-directory targets such as symlinks through no-follow attributes, requires atomic directory renames for backup, installation, and rollback instead of accepting a weaker move fallback, and forces affected parent-directory metadata after each rename.
- Closed RocksDB snapshot installation now uses a deterministic backup directory and idempotent interrupted-swap recovery: a backup-only crash state restores the prior database before startup opens the partition, while a target-plus-backup state retains the installed target and removes the stale backup; the classpath partition state machine invokes recovery before `RocksDB.open`.
- Closed RocksDB snapshot installation and interrupted-swap recovery now run behind a no-follow sidecar file lock plus an in-JVM monitor, serializing compliant server processes and threads so backup, activation, rollback, and recovery directory mutations for one database cannot interleave.
- RocksDB snapshot temporary-directory cleanup now treats only a true missing path as absent and uses no-follow attributes before walking, so a failing installer cannot leave behind a broken symlink by replacing its work directory before the finally block runs.
- RocksDB snapshot rollback, stale-backup removal, and temporary-directory cleanup now delete through a post-order file-tree visitor instead of collecting and sorting every path first, keeping recursive cleanup memory proportional to traversal depth while preserving broken-symlink removal.
- Classpath partition state-machine startup now durably prepares a fresh RocksDB directory after interrupted-snapshot recovery and before `RocksDB.open`, forcing newly created ancestry instead of relying on plain directory creation before the database begins writing state.
- Classpath Ratis partition-server startup now durably prepares its segmented-log storage directory before choosing `FORMAT` or `RECOVER`, forcing newly created ancestry before Apache Ratis begins writing consensus state.
- Real classpath data-partition Ratis state machines now implement snapshot lifecycle hooks: RocksDB opens during Ratis initialization, snapshots publish bounded deterministic single-file checkpoint archives durably, follower reinitialization extracts into fsynced staging and atomically activates a closed database directory, and installed snapshot indexes must match persisted RocksDB `lastApplied`; archive admission and extraction reject filesystem drift, non-regular checkpoint files, oversized file counts or byte totals, malformed or escaping paths, duplicates, truncation, growth, CRC32C mismatches, and trailing bytes before activation.
- Adapter health probes and health/RPC socket servers/transports now centralize socket-timeout conversion and reject negative, zero, sub-millisecond, oversized, or millisecond-overflow durations during construction before socket startup or request handling.
- Adapter health/RPC socket servers now centralize listener startup cleanup, release failed-bind listeners while remaining retryable, validate request concurrency before allocating accept executors, and retain stable accept-loop listener references while concurrent close clears the published socket.
- Adapter health/RPC socket servers now centralize shutdown cleanup, continue listener and executor draining across close failures, report shutdown timeout/interruption failures, preserve interrupt status, and make health-server close release `awaitClosed()` callers even if close happens before start.
- Adapter health/RPC socket-server shutdown now applies one monotonic close budget across accept, request, and optional operation executor drains, including forced termination checks, preventing sequential executor phases from multiplying the configured stop timeout.
- Adapter health/RPC socket-server shutdown now tracks accepted sockets through request completion and closes them during listener shutdown, including accept-close races, so partial idle requests cannot survive close until their normal read deadline; RPC transports normalize malformed or truncated peer responses as I/O failures.
- Adapter socket health probes now apply one monotonic timeout budget across connection, response read, and retry delay, preventing a failed attempt from adding an unconditional retry sleep beyond the supervisor deadline.
- Adapter socket startup-health probes now retry transient `STARTING` snapshots within that same monotonic deadline, return terminal snapshots immediately, and retain the last startup message on timeout instead of killing a legitimately initializing adapter after its first decoded health response.
- Adapter socket RPC servers now bound handler future completion and return correlated `ADAPTER_TIMEOUT` responses for stuck adapter operations before they can occupy request workers indefinitely.
- Adapter socket RPC servers now isolate synchronous handler invocation behind a bounded operation executor with one monotonic handler budget, cancellation, shutdown draining, and correlated `BUSY` responses when timed-out synchronous operations retain all slots.
- Timed-out or interrupted adapter RPC work now publishes operation-capacity risk before cancellation, so health reports degraded immediately when interrupt-ignoring canceled tasks retain every bounded operation slot without waiting for another client request to be rejected, then recovers automatically after retained workers drain.
- Adapter socket RPC timeout cancellation now remains attached while synchronous handlers publish their response futures: a timed-out or interrupted request cancels a late-published response immediately, and handler response mapping propagates cancellation to the underlying partition or metadata operation future instead of abandoning cancellable work after releasing the request-worker slot.
- Adapter socket RPC servers now publish request-worker failure before rethrowing an unchecked or fatal late-response cancellation fault from the operation worker, so an already-canceled executor wrapper cannot swallow the fault while the endpoint continues reporting healthy or accepting traffic.
- Adapter socket RPC servers now degrade typed health after an actual operation-executor rejection while all operation slots remain active and automatically recover readiness when capacity returns, allowing the external-process supervisor to observe persistent operation saturation without misclassifying ordinary full utilization.
- Adapter RPC handlers now reserve `BAD_REQUEST` for caller-controlled validation failures and report null adapter contract results, including missing partition-operation commits or payloads, as correlated `ADAPTER_ERROR` responses; process adapter client factories also classify null transport results, mismatched response IDs, and operation-incompatible successful response shapes, including non-empty metadata-reload acknowledgements, as typed `ADAPTER_ERROR` contract failures; socket RPC servers classify post-decode runtime faults as adapter failures instead of blaming the caller.
- Adapter health responses are now supplier-driven, and the classpath adapter entrypoint reports degraded health when its configured RPC listener is no longer running instead of remaining supervisor-ready with a dead RPC endpoint.
- Adapter health-supplier invocation now runs behind a bounded operation executor with acceptance-time deadlines, cancellation, shutdown draining, typed timeout or saturation degradation, and capped 100-millisecond degraded-response writes, preventing a stuck health computation from indefinitely retaining every request worker.
- Adapter process-supervisor shutdown timeouts are now converted and validated during lifecycle construction, rejecting negative, positive sub-millisecond, or millisecond-overflow values before process launch while preserving explicit zero-timeout immediate escalation.
- Adapter process-supervisor normal shutdown and failed-start health rollback now share one graceful termination clock plus a separate capped 100 milliseconds of forceful-termination confirmation grace, preserving asynchronous kill confirmation even in explicit zero-timeout immediate-escalation mode without silently doubling the configured stop timeout.
- Adapter process-supervisor failed-start health rollback now preserves the primary startup failure across checked, unchecked, or fatal termination-cleanup faults and retains ownership of a still-running launched child, allowing later lifecycle close calls to retry cleanup instead of losing the process handle.
- Adapter process-supervisor startup now rechecks child liveness after a ready health snapshot, rejecting a child that exited during health verification before public endpoints can accept traffic against a dead adapter.
- Public API server Ratis lifecycle cleanup now marks delegated cleanup complete only after success, allowing transient adapter-close failures to be retried by later server close calls. Startup rollback suppresses checked or runtime close failures onto the primary startup fault and leaves cleanup retryable.
- Public endpoint shutdown and Ratis lifecycle cleanup now share an aggregated close boundary, so endpoint cleanup failures cannot skip adapter close; the endpoint failure remains primary, any adapter-close failure is suppressed, and failed adapter cleanup remains retryable.
- Public endpoint startup and Ratis lifecycle startup now share a rollback boundary that stops already-constructed endpoint resources before adapter cleanup when either startup phase fails, suppresses endpoint or adapter rollback failures onto the original startup fault, and leaves failed adapter cleanup retryable.
- Shared Ratis lifecycle ownership now tracks delegated cleanup obligation independently from active endpoint references, so a failed first delegate start publishes no reference while endpoint-first rollback and later close calls can retry cleanup. Final endpoint release also publishes zero active references before delegated shutdown, preventing a failed adapter close from masquerading as a live endpoint; reacquisition fails closed while cleanup remains pending and becomes available again after cleanup succeeds.
- Closed Ratis-backed public server instances now reject later endpoint start attempts before accepting traffic instead of silently skipping adapter startup through stale per-instance started state.
- gRPC public endpoint shutdown now drains the gRPC server, Netty event-loop group, and request scheduler as independent aggregated resource phases before adapter cleanup. Forced server termination and event-loop termination are bounded and fail explicitly when they cannot drain; scheduler graceful-disposal failures force disposal and propagate without skipping later cleanup.
- gRPC public endpoints now track request-scheduler ownership explicitly: internal database connections lend their scheduler without transferring ownership, so endpoint shutdown or failed-start rollback leaves database executors running, while endpoint-created schedulers are still disposed during cleanup.
- Thrift public endpoint shutdown now owns an injected request-worker executor, explicitly bounds the library worker drain, and aggregates listener stop, bounded serve-thread termination, and worker-executor cleanup before adapter release. Close-before-start terminates the owned pool, constructor rollback closes partially acquired resources, and closed Thrift endpoints reject later starts.
- Shared read/write scheduler forced disposal now aggregates both scheduler and both executor cleanup phases so one failure cannot skip later teardown, bounded-waits again after `shutdownNow()`, preserves interruption, and fails explicitly if an executor still cannot terminate.
- RocksDB statistics collection now treats interrupted sampling sleep as a terminal collector stop, restores the interrupt flag, and still preserves bounded close timeout/interruption behavior.
- The classpath adapter entrypoint now registers runtime, legacy partition clients, RPC server, and health server in a reverse-order resource scope as they are created, so partial construction and shutdown failures continue draining later resources and aggregate close exceptions instead of leaking the remaining adapter resources.
- The classpath adapter reverse-order resource scope now attempts every owned close across `Error`s, clears ownership, preserves the first fatal fault, and suppresses later cleanup failures instead of stranding sibling adapter resources after partial construction rollback or shutdown failure.
- Classpath adapter-runtime startup, live reload rollback, destructive partition removal, and terminal close now aggregate checked exceptions, runtime exceptions, and `Error`s across partition clients and servers, attempt every sibling resource, clear unpublished ownership, preserve the primary fault, and suppress later failures. Partition-server failed-start rollback also closes the built Ratis server across fatal startup faults before rethrowing.
- Classpath partition servers now publish their built Ratis server only after successful startup and close failed built servers before rethrowing, keeping wrappers retryable after occupied-port bind failures instead of retaining an unreachable failed server.
- Classpath partition-server close now retains its published `RaftServer` handle until cleanup succeeds, and adapter-runtime startup or reload staging publishes wrappers into rollback maps before invoking `start()`, so transient close faults and failed-start rollback close faults remain reachable for retry.
- Classpath adapter runtimes now retain failed retired client and server cleanup obligations in private queues after route unpublication and publish partition-client closed state only after underlying close succeeds, so later fail-closed shutdown retries cleanup without republishing stale routes.
- Classpath adapter-runtime startup now drains privately retained cleanup obligations before constructing fresh resources, so restart stays stopped and publishes no new routes while older retired cleanup still fails.
- Classpath adapter-runtime reload now treats staged-add rollback cleanup failure as fail-closed: it retires previously published routes, retries the retained staged resource during shutdown, preserves the original reload failure, and suppresses the rollback close fault.
- Adapter RPC handlers now normalize synchronous collaborator runtime exceptions and `Error`s into bounded `ADAPTER_ERROR` responses, and classpath metadata reload callbacks return failed futures for fatal faults, preserving the asynchronous response contract for socket-backed and direct callers.
- Adapter-side RPC request workers now retain unexpected unchecked or fatal processing faults as degraded RPC health for the separate health endpoint, stop RPC running state, close the RPC listener to wake `accept()`, release the port, and reject restart of the failed instance; health request-worker `Error`s stop the health listener, wake `accept()`, release `awaitClosed()`, and fail the adapter process visibly instead of leaving a live-looking endpoint behind a dead worker.
- RPC and health worker-failure listener close now aggregate checked, unchecked, and fatal cleanup faults, retaining the worker failure as primary and suppressing listener-close faults so abnormal teardown cannot replace the initiating diagnostic.
- RPC and health listener startup now publishes the bound listener before accept-executor handoff, terminal accept-loop failures close their stable local listeners immediately and release failed ports before owner cleanup, and terminal failure publication remains lock-free so synchronized shutdown cannot form a monitor cycle with executor drain.
- Gateway RPC cancellation now closes the active socket from a cancellation-aware response future's `cancel()` override after ordinary pending bookkeeping, so unexpected unchecked or fatal cleanup faults propagate synchronously to the canceling caller instead of disappearing inside an ignored completion-stage dependent. Gateway cleanup retains sockets privately until `Socket.isClosed()` confirms completion across cancellation, fail-closed shutdown, retry draining, and worker unwind, so checked close failures keep a transport-shutdown retry handle. Transport shutdown drains retained sockets before and after request-worker termination and reports incomplete cleanup if any obligation remains.
- Gateway RPC transport shutdown now uses that same retained cleanup queue for pending-request active-socket close failures, so caller futures fail promptly while repeated transport close attempts retain cleanup ownership after completion bookkeeping removes pending requests.
- Gateway health-probe retries now preserve interruption as the primary abort signal, restore the caller thread's interrupt flag, and retain the transport failure that triggered an interrupted retry delay as suppressed diagnostic context.
- Shared listener startup rollback now aggregates checked, unchecked, and fatal listener-close faults, retaining the bind or accept-executor handoff failure as primary and suppressing rollback-close faults while still attempting listener release before startup returns.
- Health request workers now own accepted sockets with try-with-resources, so unchecked or fatal socket-close faults stop the health listener and become visible through `awaitClosed()`; if processing and socket close both fail, the processing fault remains primary and the close fault is suppressed.
- Shared socket writes now dispatch timeout close attempts from the single deadline scheduler onto daemon virtual threads, so one slow socket close cannot delay later write deadlines. A timed writer grants its own close attempt up to 100 milliseconds of diagnostic grace before returning: completed checked, unchecked, and fatal timeout-close faults remain suppressed on the primary `SocketTimeoutException`, while an unfinished close contributes an explicit suppressed incomplete-cleanup diagnostic instead of pinning the writer indefinitely.
- Running classpath adapter-runtime reloads now unpublish removed resources as they close and fail closed when live state-machine reload or destructive removal cleanup fails, preventing partially reloaded or already-closed resources from remaining published; non-destructive addition failures retain rollback behavior.
- Classpath adapter-runtime RPC resolution now returns runtime-owned borrowed clients backed by per-partition usage slots: removal retires the slot before close, stale borrowed handles fail closed after unpublication, and underlying client close waits for work that already acquired that partition without serializing unrelated partitions.
- Classpath adapter runtimes now expose typed health snapshots, and the adapter process health endpoint composes runtime health with RPC-listener health so fail-closed reloads stop advertising supervisor readiness even while the RPC listener remains open.
- Classpath Ratis partition clients now replace Ratis builder default infinite no-sleep retries with bounded 3-second request/watch attempts, three attempts, and 100-millisecond retry sleeps whose 9.2-second maximum envelope fits below the adapter RPC deadline.
- Adapter socket RPC servers now retain the first unexpected accept-loop failure, report sanitized degraded health with the listener cause, and reject in-place restart of a failed running server while preserving retry after a pre-start bind failure; the classpath adapter health endpoint serves this typed RPC health snapshot.
- Adapter socket health servers now reject in-place restart after an unexpected accept-loop failure has been recorded, preserving their one-shot latch and executor lifecycle while retaining retry after a pre-start bind failure.
- Shared adapter socket body readers now refresh `SO_TIMEOUT` from one monotonic read budget for RPC requests, RPC responses, health requests, and health responses, preventing slow-drip peers from retaining sockets and request workers indefinitely by resetting a full timeout for each byte or chunk.
- Adapter RPC requests, RPC responses, busy replies, health requests, and health responses now share monotonic socket-write budgets that close timed-out sockets from one daemon deadline executor, preventing peers that stop reading from retaining transport calls or request workers indefinitely.
- Adapter socket RPC servers now apply one acceptance-time budget across request read, decoded-request admission, handler invocation, future completion, and successful response write; expired decoded requests do not enter adapter execution, and small error or direct `BUSY` replies receive only a capped 100-millisecond write grace.
- Adapter socket health servers now check the accepted request's monotonic budget before submitting health-supplier work. Already-expired requests return a degraded timeout response without entering the supplier executor; budget exhaustion in the narrow post-check race cancels the admitted invocation and returns the same degraded timeout response.
- Adapter RPC models now cap binary payloads at 47 MiB before defensive copying, leaving more than 1 MiB for the line envelope after Base64 expansion inside the default 64 MiB socket frame. Codec decode rejects oversized encoded payload text before Base64 allocation; request IDs, partition IDs, error types, and error messages are bounded and reject NUL or any line separator recognized by the codec's Java `\R` grammar; oversized adapter failure messages are normalized into a bounded streaming buffer before correlated error-response construction; operation-specific shape validation runs before cloning malformed request or response payloads; and process clients no longer clone proposal or query payloads once before the request model performs its ownership copy.
- Shared adapter socket framing now encodes and decodes strict UTF-8 instead of replacement-mode text conversion, rejecting malformed local Unicode before connection and malformed RPC or health peer bytes before protocol parsing while preserving typed server-side failure responses.
- Shared adapter socket framing now preflights exact UTF-8 byte length before allocating encoded output, rejecting oversized multibyte text before full materialization. RPC and health text share Java `\R`-aligned line safety, and health-server failure messages use the same bounded streaming normalization as RPC errors.
- Shared adapter socket readers now collect inbound RPC bodies and health request lines in cap-aware buffers whose backing storage never grows beyond the configured frame limit, then strict-decode directly from those buffers without a full-frame `toByteArray()` duplicate.
- Gateway adapter RPC transports now submit socket connect/write/read work to bounded zero-core daemon executors whose idle workers expire, returning immediately for accepted work and failing the returned future promptly when client capacity is saturated instead of blocking callers or creating unbounded sockets.
- Public `ReplicatedConnection` async single-request routing now uses a connection-owned bounded zero-core daemon executor whose idle workers expire instead of the JVM common pool. Saturation fails returned futures promptly; caller cancellation interrupts the owned worker so blocked partition submission can cancel its active gateway future; close publishes terminal admission state once, independently tracks executor shutdown, owned resolver cleanup, and bounded termination confirmation, retries only unfinished phases after cleanup faults, and rejects later sync or async submissions.
- Canceling a gateway adapter RPC future now closes its registered active socket across connect, write, or response-read handling, releasing bounded client-worker capacity promptly instead of retaining it until the RPC timeout.
- Process client-factory response validation and public proposal, commit-wait, read, or metadata-reload mappings now propagate cancellation through every future layer to the gateway transport future, so public cancellation reaches active-socket cleanup instead of stopping at an intermediate `thenApply`.
- Synchronous metadata command proposal commit waits, committed snapshot queries, and live adapter metadata reloads now await active futures interruptibly, cancel them before propagating interruption, and restore the thread interrupt flag, so public async cancellation can release socket-backed metadata work promptly.
- Experimental `ClusterConnection` replica-read failover now retains cancellation ownership across changing endpoint attempts: canceling the caller-visible future cancels the active or late-published endpoint request, stops normal retry progression, and preserves fatal endpoint cancellation cleanup faults for the canceling caller.
- Public gRPC future bridges now propagate caller cancellation through response error mapping to underlying reactive or Guava RPC futures. Guava response-mapper failures complete the caller-visible future exceptionally before fatal callback faults are rethrown, so unexpected response handling cannot strand callers.
- Shared async composition now preserves cancellation while its active stage changes: merge-operator ensure cancels its remote existence check or later upload, including late upload publication, and large no-result gRPC put or merge chunking preserves cancellation through its final empty-list mapping.
- Synchronous gRPC single-request routing now waits interruptibly: interruption cancels the active RPC future before returning and restores the thread interrupt flag, while runtime and fatal asynchronous RPC failures retain direct propagation.
- The legacy Thrift client async boundary now uses one bounded daemon worker for its stateful framed transport, cancellation-aware async submissions, cancelable `getRangeAsync` stream workers, and `shutdownNow()` plus bounded termination confirmation on close; unterminated or interrupted worker cleanup is reported while transport/base cleanup still runs, and synchronous entry points serialize shared client access for mixed sync/async callers. Thrift key-level delete/deleteMulti sync and async calls now fail closed with `NOT_IMPLEMENTED` because the generated Thrift transport only exposes column deletion.
- Resolver close now propagates through the process client factory to gateway RPC transport shutdown, stopping the client executor, closing registered active sockets, failing pending futures promptly, and rejecting later sends instead of retaining RPC work until socket deadlines or worker expiry.
- Gateway adapter RPC transport shutdown now bounded-waits for client executor termination after interrupting workers, closing registered active sockets, and failing pending futures, so resolver close returns only after gateway socket work has drained or fails explicitly.
- Repeated gateway adapter RPC transport close calls now rerun shutdown and bounded executor drain, so an interrupted or timed-out cleanup attempt remains retryable while later submissions stay rejected.
- Gateway adapter RPC transport shutdown now aggregates unchecked faults across executor interruption, each pending request's active-socket close and exceptional future completion, and the bounded executor drain, so one abnormal socket close cannot prevent sibling futures from failing or skip executor termination verification.
- Gateway adapter RPC submission now completes its registered response exceptionally before rethrowing an unexpected client-executor handoff runtime exception or `Error`, so normal completion cleanup removes the otherwise unreachable pending request.
- Repeated adapter-side RPC and health socket server close calls now rerun listener shutdown and bounded executor drain, so an interrupted or timed-out cleanup attempt remains retryable while the endpoints stay terminal for new traffic.
- Shared adapter socket listener teardown now aggregates checked exceptions, runtime exceptions, and `Error`s across listener close, accepted-socket close, and accept/request/operation executor phases, preserving the first failure while still draining sibling resources.
- Accepted adapter socket registries now attempt every tracked socket across checked, unchecked, and fatal close faults, remove successful closes, and retain abnormal close failures so repeated endpoint shutdown retries unfinished cleanup. Registration races with shutdown follow the same retain-on-failure rule, and owned registry cleanup no longer reuses the quiet writer-timeout close helper.
- RPC and health request workers now remove accepted sockets from that registry only after `Socket.isClosed()` confirms worker cleanup completed, retaining an owner-shutdown retry handle when fatal try-with-resources socket close fails.
- RPC request-executor saturation BUSY responses and health request-executor rejection closes now keep accepted sockets registered until `Socket.isClosed()` confirms cleanup completed, retaining an owner-shutdown retry handle after abnormal rejection-path close faults.
- Unexpected RPC and health request-executor handoff failures now keep accepted sockets registered through cleanup, preserving socket-close faults as suppressed handoff diagnostics while retaining owner-shutdown retry ownership until `Socket.isClosed()` confirms completion.
- Accepted-socket registration racing owner shutdown now tracks newly accepted sockets before immediate close, leaves them registered if close fails, and exposes no unconditional registry deregistration API, so a later owner close can retry cleanup.
- RPC and health owner shutdown now retain listening-socket handles until `ServerSocket.isClosed()` confirms cleanup, so a later owner close retries listener cleanup that failed before completion while each shutdown pass still drains accepted sockets and executors.
- RPC and health startup now publish listener handles before bind and retain them when rollback close does not complete. Completed rollback still permits retry; incomplete rollback rejects restart until owner close retries cleanup, preventing handle overwrite.
- Adapter-side RPC and health listener startup now closes newly bound sockets and resets endpoint running state when accept-loop handoff throws a runtime exception or `Error`, preserving same-port startup retry without a leaked listener.
- Adapter-side RPC and health accept loops now deregister and close just-accepted sockets, clear running state, and publish terminal listener failure when request-executor handoff throws an unexpected runtime exception or `Error`, instead of leaking sockets behind a dead accept worker.
- Process adapter partition clients now publish refresh-driven close state across threads, so stale client references retained during resolver refresh reject later submissions consistently. The bounded transport constructor also rejects non-positive concurrency limits.
- Adapter-backed resolver refresh now publishes its replacement client map before best-effort stale-client cleanup, logging runtime or fatal cleanup faults without reporting a committed route refresh as failed. Failed staged or stale cleanup remains retained for later retry, and terminal resolver close tracks client and factory cleanup completion independently so repeated close retries unfinished work without repeating successful phases. Resolver construction failure invokes the same complete close path, giving retained staged clients a final cleanup retry before the failed resolver becomes unreachable.
- Process-backed replicated-connection construction now centralizes resolver ownership transfer. If final connection assembly fails after resolver creation, the resolver closes before the failure escapes, draining partition clients and the adapter factory while attaching any cleanup fault as suppressed context on the primary construction failure.
- Adapter health snapshots now enforce an explicit 256-character diagnostic-message cap at model construction and decode boundaries. Health servers normalize the final degraded response after composing context, so long unsupported requests cannot expand a typed health reply beyond that budget and the listener remains available for later probes.
- Adapter startup-health probes now treat malformed health codec responses and invalid UTF-8 peer frames as terminal typed I/O failures, failing closed promptly instead of leaking unchecked codec exceptions or retrying protocol-invalid responses as transient socket noise.
- Adapter RPC and health codecs now stream Java `\R` line iteration instead of allocating regex split arrays and reject unknown keys before retaining them, preventing newline-heavy or unknown-field-heavy bounded frames from expanding parser-side allocation before fail-closed admission.
- Adapter RPC and health codecs now parse source ranges, match allowed keys without copying, enforce field-specific character budgets before value substring creation, and bound parser diagnostics for malformed lines or unknown keys.
- Adapter RPC and health codecs now reject duplicate keys immediately after key admission and before replacement-value sizing or substring creation, avoiding unnecessary retention for duplicate payload or diagnostic fields.
- Adapter RPC and health codecs now reject leading, interior, or repeated empty logical lines instead of silently skipping separators that canonical encoders never produce, while retaining one trailing separator compatibility.
- Adapter RPC and health codecs now bound `=` discovery to each admitted source range, so a malformed head line fails without scanning a later hostile tail for separators.
- Adapter health request framing now requires an explicit newline terminator and rejects EOF-truncated lines before dispatch while returning a bounded degraded response and keeping the listener available for later probes.
- A shared endpoint URI validator now backs `ClusterNodeMetadata`, `ReplicaMember`, `BootstrapPeer`, modeled `RegisterNode` metadata commands, bootstrap manifest loading, and generated adapter metadata loading; node and bootstrap peer metadata validate bare public endpoint shape and bare `grpc` Ratis endpoint shape at construction and command payload decode boundaries, while replica members validate bare endpoint shape before entering partition or route-group metadata.
- Partition descriptors now reject duplicate peer node IDs before metadata-command, workflow-planner, generated metadata, or adapter config paths can consume ambiguous partition membership.
- Peer-change metadata commands now reject duplicate peer node IDs at command construction and decode boundaries, so malformed membership changes fail before applier-side descriptor construction.
- Partition descriptors and peer-change metadata commands now require non-null leader hints to target voting peers, so learner-only or learner-led metadata cannot enter descriptor construction, command decode, or membership-change application paths.
- Replica member metadata now rejects impossible progress state where `appliedIndex` is greater than `matchIndex`, preventing route selection, catch-up policy, or generated adapter metadata from consuming a replica that claims to have applied unmatched log entries.
- Cluster metadata now validates paired partition descriptors and route groups together: when both maps contain the same partition ID, the route group peers and leader must match the descriptor peers and leader hint before route caches or adapter metadata generators can consume the snapshot.
- Partition membership add-replica planning now rejects unregistered learner targets when the metadata snapshot carries registered nodes, so operator workflows fail before emitting peer-change commands that the metadata applier would reject later.
- Node registration, cluster-node metadata, bootstrap peers, and replicated-mode config now share failure-domain validation that rejects line-broken labels before replicated metadata or generated config materialization.
- Core node IDs now reject line-broken values at construction, including values loaded from persisted node identity or generated adapter metadata, while preserving existing literal identifier characters such as `/` for storage-layout escaping.
- Core partition Ratis group IDs now reject line-broken values at descriptor construction, workflow planning, and generated metadata loading before they can reach metadata-command or adapter config materialization.
- Partition placement constraint keys and values now reject blank or line-broken labels at descriptor construction, workflow planning, metadata-command decode, and generated metadata loading before they can reach adapter config materialization.
- Core column names now reject line-broken values at model construction, metadata-command decode, generated metadata loading, and metadata state-machine application.
- Merge-operator schema names and classes now reject blank or line-broken values at model construction, metadata-command decode, generated metadata loading, and metadata state-machine application.
- A classpath Ratis metadata group is now proven behind the adapter boundary: the metadata snapshot format preserves replica groups, a metadata state machine applies committed commands once by log index, the metadata submitter proposes through a `RatisPartitionClient` and queries the committed snapshot, and the classpath probe drives `ReplicatedConnection` create-column, register-node, and create-partition metadata workflows through a real Ratis group.
- Configured adapter launch plans now add a bootstrap-manifest-derived internal metadata group descriptor before data partition descriptors, the classpath state-machine factory routes that descriptor to the metadata state machine, and configured public `ReplicatedConnection` instances inject a Ratis metadata submitter from the adapter-backed resolver.
- The classpath metadata-group state machine now has a Ratis snapshot hook: the probe writes a committed metadata snapshot, restarts the snapshot peer with deliberately invalid initial metadata, and reads the created column plus partition back from the snapshot-backed state machine.
- The metadata-group no-snapshot startup path now fails fast for generated live metadata with already-applied state through the actual classpath metadata state-machine initialization path against Ratis storage. The only accepted no-snapshot seed is bootstrap-shaped metadata: epoch 0, no user metadata, and any carried replica groups still at zero term/config/progress with serving voters. This prevents unsafe replay against advanced metadata; it does not yet provide an availability-complete restart path after metadata mutation unless a Ratis snapshot exists.

## Blocking Items

- Ratis cannot be placed directly on the production JPMS module path because the selected Ratis artifacts still have split-package automatic-module issues.
- Full native-image support is not proven and currently fails during baseline native-image analysis before Ratis-specific native behavior can be validated.
- Direct Ratis project dependencies are not accepted for JPMS/fatjar/runtime distribution. The selected `ratis-shaded-adapter` package path starts the production-candidate artifact while keeping Ratis out of Rockserver core's JPMS path and relocating `org.apache.ratis` inside the adapter jar. The inactive `ratis-spike` package lifecycle remains the runtime rehearsal baseline by copying Ratis artifacts only into the isolated adapter distribution and extracting Ratis/RainbowGum notice metadata beside it.
