A configured deployment preflight now checks real launch plans for a directory-backed adapter home, executable/classpath files, classpath real-path containment under the real adapter home, duplicate command/distribution classpath entries and symlink aliases, writable generated config/metadata and data directories outside the adapter home, symlink-aware non-overlapping storage and planned-file paths, listener separation including local Ratis peer listener collisions and loopback host aliases, and peer endpoint shape, while command, distribution, and manifest parsing reject normalized root, directory, non-JAR runtime classpath entries, non-portable environment keys, Java launcher environment overrides, native loader environment overrides, line-unsafe launch paths, and Ratis peer descriptors reject non-`grpc` endpoints before adapter startup. The preflight path writes a structured `ratis-adapter-preflight.properties` report and requires accepted preflight before any `ratis-adapter-evidence.properties` launch-evidence construction or atomically published artifact with command, environment, listener/storage paths, launch-file SHA-256/size fingerprints, and canonical generated config/metadata hashes streamed into SHA-256 for supervisor archival, but production acceptance still requires copied/shaded notice review, deployed-environment supervision, and network/storage rehearsal; see `REPLICATION_RATIS_PACKAGING_DECISION.md`.
- Single-partition Ratis-backed put/get/delete, local external-process adapter restart, and a classpath Ratis metadata-group state machine/client are proven in the isolated classpath probe; modeled public metadata commands can now be encoded, constructed, proposed through a configured adapter-boundary Ratis submitter, consumed through the refresh path, and recovered from a metadata-group Ratis snapshot in the classpath probe. Initial partition creation now has a workflow planner that derives voting replica metadata from registered nodes, `CreatePartition` commits both adapter-consumed partition descriptors and route groups, and `ReplicatedConnection` can submit `registerNode(...)` plus `createPartition(...)` metadata commands through the injected submitter before routing a subsequent write after refresh. Generated-live metadata has an explicit startup policy requiring a Ratis snapshot.

## Unsupported API Gate

The replicated public boundary must keep rejecting:

- local transaction handles, update handles, iterator handles, and embedded CDC handles;
- local-only admin/raw-scan paths that cannot be represented as deterministic replicated commands;
- `*_NO_WAL`, direct SST ingestion, ingest-behind, and other embedded durability shortcuts;
- local `mergeOperatorClass` schema shortcuts that bypass replicated metadata activation;
- new merge-operator artifact uploads until replicated artifact distribution and activation preflight are implemented;
- cross-partition multi-key writes in the first production cut.

Verification commands:

- `mvn -Dtest=ReplicatedCommandClassifierTest test`
- `mvn -Dtest=ReplicatedGrpcBoundaryTest,ReplicatedThriftBoundaryTest test`
- `mvn -Dtest=ReplicatedWriteSyncAPITest test`

## Bootstrap Runbook

1. Prepare identical bootstrap manifests on every initial metadata peer.
2. Verify the manifest contains the cluster ID, metadata group ID, initial peer IDs, public API endpoints, Ratis endpoints, failure domains, and bootstrap epoch.
3. Start each initial node with stable local storage and replicated mode enabled.
4. Verify each node persists its node ID and cluster ID before joining any metadata or data group.
5. Form the metadata group from the bootstrap manifest and reject any node with mismatched identity or duplicate endpoint configuration.
6. Commit initial node and partition metadata through metadata commands.
7. Verify route-cache epoch, metadata epoch, and public/Ratis endpoint separation before accepting client traffic.

Abort conditions:

- any node reports a different cluster ID;
- public and Ratis endpoints are identical;
- initial metadata peers disagree on manifest content;
- a node has stale local identity from another cluster.

## Deployed Adapter Rehearsal Runbook

1. Build the adapter distribution from the accepted Maven target, starting with `mvn -P library,ratis-shaded-adapter -DskipTests package` for the selected shaded artifact, and publish the identical adapter home to every rehearsal host.
2. Prepare stable data, metadata, generated-config, and adapter working directories on each host; do not place them on overlapping mount points.
3. Install the identical bootstrap manifest and replicated-mode configuration on every initial metadata peer, with host-local health/RPC listener ports distinct from the local Ratis endpoint.
4. Run the configured deployment preflight on every node and archive `ratis-adapter-preflight.properties` plus the generated `ratis-adapter-evidence.properties` file containing the launch command, environment, generated config path, generated metadata path, listeners, storage paths, launch-file SHA-256/size fingerprints, and canonical generated config/metadata hashes before supervisor startup.
5. Start each adapter process under the selected supervisor and verify the supervisor uses the generated launch command without adding classpath, module-path, `-jar`, Java agent, boot-classpath, patch-module, launcher inspection/dry-run, or config overrides.
6. Verify each adapter reports versioned `READY`, binds only the declared health/RPC/Ratis listeners, and writes no state outside the approved data/metadata/working directories.
7. Through the public replicated connection, commit node registration, create a column, create a partition, and perform put/get through the generated adapter distribution.
8. Transfer or stop the current leader, verify writes continue through a new leader, restart the stopped process with retained storage, and verify catch-up reads from the restarted peer.
9. Verify generated metadata refresh publishes new config/metadata files atomically, triggers adapter `RELOAD_METADATA`, and refreshes public routing only after reload succeeds.
10. Archive supervisor logs, adapter logs, metadata snapshots, Ratis storage paths, generated notice metadata, command output, and final public API validation output.

Abort conditions:

- any node preflight report is rejected;
- supervisor launch command differs from the generated launch plan;
- health/RPC/Ratis listeners collide or bind unexpected interfaces;
- generated config, generated metadata, working, and partition data paths overlap;
- public metadata commands require pre-published data partition fixtures;
- restarted peers cannot catch up from retained storage or installed snapshots.

## Node Replacement Runbook

1. Confirm the existing partition group has a healthy quorum and fresh metadata epoch.
2. Register the replacement node with stable identity, capacity report, public endpoint, Ratis endpoint, and failure domain.
3. Add the replacement as a learner or conservative replacement candidate according to the proven Ratis role path.
4. Catch up the replacement from snapshot and log replay.
5. Verify applied index, snapshot checksum, dedupe state, CDC position, and serving health.
6. Promote only after catch-up is verified.
7. Transfer leadership away from the old node when needed.
8. Remove the old voter only while old quorum safety and caught-up serving replacement leader constraints hold.
9. Verify client routes refresh to the new peer set and stale nodes cannot rejoin as voters with old metadata.

Abort conditions:

- no healthy quorum exists before replacement;
- replacement is behind the required applied index;
- placement policy would collapse failure-domain diversity;
- listener/observer catch-up is requested without a proven promotion path.

## Partition Split Runbook

1. Confirm the source partition is active, healthy, and not already in split or merge state.
2. Capture the current metadata epoch, partition epoch, route ownership, and CDC resume tokens.
3. Validate the split boundary with unsigned encoded-key ordering and bucketed-column collision rules.
4. Plan child partition descriptors, child epochs, placement, and Ratis group IDs.
5. Commit the split prepare metadata command with epoch fences.
6. Copy or snapshot the source state into child partitions according to the selected implementation path.
7. Tail committed changes until the split cut is reached.
8. Commit split activation and CDC token translation records in metadata.
9. Clear stale route-cache entries and verify writes route to child leaders.
10. Retire or archive the source partition only after child checksums and route behavior are verified.

Abort conditions:

- split boundary falls inside a bucketed collision group;
- source partition epoch changes during planning;
- child placement cannot satisfy voter policy;
- CDC token translation records cannot be committed.

## Backup Runbook

1. Select a backup scope and record the metadata epoch.
2. For each partition, request a Ratis snapshot/checkpoint cut and record the applied index.
3. Capture the RocksDB checkpoint manifest, Ratis snapshot manifest, CDC position, dedupe watermark, schema metadata, and file chunk checksums.
4. Enforce configured per-partition byte, snapshot chunk-count, partition-count, and concurrency limits.
5. Store artifacts with deterministic ordering and aggregate checksums.
6. Verify every partition has a manifest entry and every manifest checksum matches the stored artifact.
7. Record the source cluster ID, source metadata epoch, partition epochs, and snapshot cut in the audit trail.

Abort conditions:

- a partition cannot produce a stable snapshot cut;
- artifact checksums mismatch;
- a required schema/dedupe/CDC manifest entry is missing;
- the backup exceeds configured partition, snapshot chunk-count, or partition byte limits.

## Restore Runbook

1. Prefer restore into a new cluster ID unless an explicit same-cluster recovery procedure is approved.
2. Validate backup manifest checksums before creating target metadata.
3. Create target node identities and bootstrap manifest.
4. Restore metadata with new target epochs unless performing an approved same-cluster epoch-advanced recovery.
5. Install each partition snapshot into a clean partition replica path.
6. Verify schema keys, user data checksums, dedupe watermarks, CDC positions, and partition epochs.
7. Form metadata and data groups, then enable client routing only after all restored partitions pass verification.
8. Preserve the source manifest cut for audit and operator comparison.

Abort conditions:

- target cluster ID conflicts with source without an approved same-cluster recovery plan;
- any artifact checksum or partition epoch validation fails;
- target storage path is not clean;
- restored metadata would route clients to missing partition groups.

## Disaster Recovery Runbook

1. Declare whether the recovery target is a new cluster or same-cluster emergency recovery.
2. Fence surviving stale nodes from accepting replicated traffic before restore begins.
3. Select the latest backup whose manifests pass checksum validation.
4. Bootstrap replacement metadata peers and restore metadata.
5. Restore partition replicas from backup artifacts and verify applied cuts.
6. Recreate route-cache state from restored metadata; do not reuse stale client-side route hints.
7. Bring public API traffic back in read-only validation mode first when possible.
8. Enable writes only after metadata quorum, data-group quorum, snapshot checksums, dedupe watermarks, and CDC positions are verified.
9. Archive the incident restore manifest and final validation output.

Abort conditions:

- no backup passes manifest validation;
- stale old-cluster nodes can still accept client traffic;
- restored groups cannot form quorum;
- dedupe or CDC state is missing for partitions that accepted response-producing writes before the disaster.
